PPDATADIR\t_IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x90, int)\n\n/* Negotiate a particular IEEE 1284 mode. */\n#define PPNEGOT\t\t_IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x91, int)\n\n/* Set control lines when an interrupt occurs. */\n#define {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01E7uo4cR2xR8syxmU5tyCn6","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Syscall description language\n     2→\n     3→aka `syzlang` (`[siːzˈlæŋg]`)\n     4→\n     5→Pseudo-formal grammar of syscall description:\n     6→\n     7→```\n     8→syscallname \"(\" [arg [\",\" arg]*] \")\" [type] [\"(\" attribute* \")\"]\n     9→arg = argname type\n    10→argname = identifier\n    11→type = typename [ \"[\" type-options \"]\" ]\n    12→typename = \"const\" | \"intN\" | \"intptr\" | \"flags\" | \"array\" | \"ptr\" |\n    13→\t   \"string\" | \"filename\" | \"glob\" | \"len\" |\n    14→\t   \"bytesize\" | \"bytesizeN\" | \"bitsize\" | \"vma\" | \"proc\" |\n    15→\t   \"compressed_image\"\n    16→type-options = [type-opt [\",\" type-opt]]\n    17→```\n    18→\n    19→common type-options include:\n    20→\n    21→```\n    22→\"opt\" - the argument is optional (like mmap fd argument, or accept peer argument)\n    23→```\n    24→\n    25→rest of the type-options are type-specific:\n    26→\n    27→```\n    28→\"const\": integer constant, type-options:\n    29→\tvalue, underlying type (one of \"intN\", \"intptr\")\n    30→\"intN\"/\"intptr\": an integer without a particular meaning, type-options:\n    31→\teither an optional range of values (e.g. \"5:10\", or \"100:200\")\n    32→\tor a reference to flags description (see below),\n    33→\tor a single value\n    34→\toptionally followed by an alignment parameter if using a range\n    35→\"flags\": a set of values, type-options:\n    36→\treference to flags description (see below), underlying int type (e.g. \"int32\")\n    37→\"array\": a variable/fixed-length array, type-options:\n    38→\ttype of elements, optional size (fixed \"5\", or ranged \"5:10\", boundaries inclusive)\n    39→\"ptr\"/\"ptr64\": a pointer to an object, type-options:\n    40→\tdirection (in/out/inout); type of the object\n    41→\tptr64 has size of 8 bytes regardless of target pointer size\n    42→\"string\": a zero-terminated memory buffer (no pointer indirection implied), type-options:\n    43→\teither a string value in quotes for constant strings (e.g. \"foo\" or `deadbeef` for hex literal),\n    44→\tor a reference to string flags (special value `filename` produces file names),\n    45→\toptionally followed by a buffer size (string values will be padded with \\x00 to that size)\n    46→\"stringnoz\": a non-zero-terminated memory buffer (no pointer indirection implied), type-options:\n    47→\teither a string value in quotes for constant strings (e.g. \"foo\" or `deadbeef` for hex literal),\n    48→\tor a reference to string flags,\n    49→\"glob\": glob pattern to match on the target files, type-options:\n    50→\ta pattern string in quotes (syntax: https://golang.org/pkg/path/filepath/#Match)\n    51→\t(e.g. \"/sys/\" or \"/sys/**/*\"),\n    52→\tor include exclude glob too (e.g. \"/sys/**/*:-/sys/power/state\")\n    53→\"fmt\": a string representation of an integer (not zero-terminated), type-options:\n    54→\tformat (one of \"dec\", \"hex\", \"oct\") and the value (a resource, int, flags or proc)\n    55→\tthe resulting data is always fixed-size (formatted as \"%020llu\", \"0x%016llx\" or \"%023llo\", respectively)\n    56→\"len\": length of another field (for array it is number of elements), type-options:\n    57→\targname of the object\n    58→\"bytesize\": similar to \"len\", but always denotes the size in bytes, type-options:\n    59→\targname of the object\n    60→\"bitsize\": similar to \"len\", but always denotes the size in bits, type-options:\n    61→\targname of the object\n    62→\"offsetof\": offset of the field from the beginning of the parent struct, type-options:\n    63→\tfield\n    64→\"vma\"/\"vma64\": a pointer to a set of pages (used as input for mmap/munmap/mremap/madvise), type-options:\n    65→\toptional number of pages (e.g. vma[7]), or a range of pages (e.g. vma[2-4])\n    66→\tvma64 has size of 8 bytes regardless of target pointer size\n    67→\"proc\": per process int (see description below), type-options:\n    68→\tvalue range start, how many values per process, underlying type\n    69→\"compressed_image\": zlib-compressed disk image\n    70→\tsyscalls accepting compressed images must be marked with `no_generate`\n    71→\tand `no_minimize` call attributes. if the content of the decompressed image\n    72→\tcan be checked by a `fsck`-like command, use the `fsck` syscall attribute\n    73→\"text\": machine code of the specified type, type-options:\n    74→\ttext type (x86_real, x86_16, x86_32, x86_64, arm64)\n    75→\"void\": type with static size 0\n    76→\tmostly useful inside of templates and varlen unions, can't be syscall argument\n    77→```\n    78→\n    79→flags/len/flags also have trailing underlying type type-option when used in structs/unions/pointers.\n    80→\n    81→Flags are described as:\n    82→\n    83→```\n    84→flagname = const [\",\" const]*\n    85→```\n    86→\n    87→or for string flags as:\n    88→\n    89→```\n    90→flagname = \"\\\"\" literal \"\\\"\" [\",\" \"\\\"\" literal \"\\\"\"]*\n    91→```\n    92→\n    93→Call attributes are:\n    94→\n    95→```\n    96→\"disabled\": the call will not be used in fuzzing; useful to temporary disable some calls\n    97→\tor prohibit particular argument combinations.\n    98→\"timeout[N]\": additional execution timeout (in ms) for the call on top of some default value.\n    99→\"prog_timeout[N]\": additional execution timeout (in ms) for the whole program if it contains this call;\n   100→\tif a program contains several such calls, the max value is used.\n   101→\"ignore_return\": ignore return value of this syscall in fallback feedback; need to be used for calls\n   102→\tthat don't return fixed error codes but rather something else (e.g. the current time).\n   103→\"breaks_returns\": ignore return values of all subsequent calls in the program in fallback feedback (can't be trusted).\n   104→\"no_generate\": do not try to generate this syscall, i.e. use only seed descriptions to produce it.\n   105→\"no_minimize\": do not modify instances of this syscall when trying to minimize a crashing program.\n   106→\"no_squash\": do not attempt to pass squashed arguments to this syscall.\n   107→\tWithout that, the fuzzer will sometimes attempt to replace complex structures with arrays of bytes,\n   108→\tpossibly triggering interesting mutations, but also making programs hard to reason about.\n   109→\"fsck\": the content of the compressed buffer argument for this syscall is a file system and the\n   110→\tstring argument is a fsck-like command that will be called to verify the filesystem.\n   111→\"remote_cover\": wait longer to collect remote coverage for this call.\n   112→\"kfuzz_test\": the call is a kfuzztest target.\n   113→\"snapshot\": the call is enabled by default only in snapshot fuzzing mode,\n   114→\tbut \"enable_syscalls\" and \"disable_syscalls\" config parameters override this.\n   115→\tIt is generally used to mark calls that are not safe to execute in non-snapshot mode\n   116→\t(can lead to false positives, or lost connections to test machines.\n   117→```\n   118→\n   119→## Ints\n   120→\n   121→`int8`, `int16`, `int32` and `int64` denote an integer of the corresponding size.\n   122→`intptr` denotes a pointer-sized integer, i.e. C `long` type.\n   123→\n   124→By appending `be` suffix (e.g. `int16be`) integers become big-endian.\n   125→\n   126→It's possible to specify a range of values for an integer in the format of `int32[0:100]` or `int32[0:4096, 512]` for a 512-aligned int.\n   127→\n   128→Integers can also take a reference to flags description or a value as its first type-option.\n   129→In that case, the alignment parameter is not supported.\n   130→\n   131→To denote a bitfield of size N use `int64:N`.\n   132→\n   133→It's possible to use these various kinds of ints as base types for `const`, `flags`, `len` and `proc`.\n   134→\n   135→```\n   136→example_struct {\n   137→\tf0\tint8\t\t\t# random 1-byte integer\n   138→\tf1\tconst[0x42, int16be]\t# const 2-byte integer with value 0x4200 (big-endian 0x42)\n   139→\tf2\tint32[0:100]\t\t# random 4-byte integer with values from 0 to 100 inclusive\n   140→\tf3\tint32[1:10, 2]\t\t# random 4-byte integer with values {1, 3, 5, 7, 9}\n   141→\tf4\tint64:20\t\t# random 20-bit bitfield\n   142→\tf5\tint8[10]\t\t# const 1-byte integer with value 10\n   143→\tf6\tint32[flagname]\t\t# random 4-byte integer from the set of values referenced by flagname\n   144→}\n   145→```\n   146→\n   147→## Structs\n   148→\n   149→Structs are described as:\n   150→\n   151→```\n   152→structname \"{\" \"\\n\"\n   153→\t(fieldname type (\"(\" fieldattribute* \")\")? (if[expression])? \"\\n\")+\n   154→\"}\" (\"[\" attribute* \"]\")?\n   155→```\n   156→\n   157→Fields can have attributes specified in parentheses after the field, independent\n   158→of their type. `in/out/inout` attribute specify per-field direction, for example:\n   159→\n   160→```\n   161→foo {\n   162→\tfield0\tconst[1, int32]\t(in)\n   163→\tfield1\tint32\t\t(inout)\n   164→\tfield2\tfd\t\t(out)\n   165→}\n   166→```\n   167→\n   168→You may specify conditions that determine whether a field will be included:\n   169→\n   170→```\n   171→foo {\n   172→\tfield0\tint32\n   173→\tfield1\tint32 (if[value[field0] == 0x1])\n   174→}\n   175→```\n   176→\n   177→See [the corresponding section](syscall_descriptions_syntax.md#conditional-fields)\n   178→for more details.\n   179→\n   180→`out_overlay` attribute allows to have separate input and output layouts for the struct.\n   181→Fields before the `out_overlay` field are input, fields starting from `out_overlay` are output.\n   182→Input and output fields overlap in memory (both start from the beginning of the struct in memory).\n   183→For example:\n   184→\n   185→```\n   186→foo {\n   187→\tin0\tconst[1, int32]\n   188→\tin1\tflags[bar, int8]\n   189→\tin2\tptr[in, string]\n   190→\tout0\tfd\t(out_overlay)\n   191→\tout1\tint32\n   192→}\n   193→```\n   194→\n   195→Structs can have attributes specified in square brackets after the struct.\n   196→Attributes are:\n   197→\n   198→- `packed`: the struct does not have paddings between fields and has alignment 1; this is similar to GNU C `__attribute__((packed))`; struct alignment can be overridden with `align` attribute\n   199→- `align[N]`: the struct has alignment N and padded up to multiple of `N`; contents of the padding are unspecified (though, frequently are zeros); similar to GNU C `__attribute__((aligned(N)))`\n   200→- `size[N]`: the struct is padded up to the specified size `N`; contents of the padding are unspecified (though, frequently are zeros)\n   201→\n   202→## Unions\n   203→\n   204→Unions are described as:\n   205→\n   206→```\n   207→unionname \"[\" \"\\n\"\n   208→\t(fieldname type (if[expression])? \"\\n\")+\n   209→\"]\" (\"[\" attribute* \"]\")?\n   210→```\n   211→\n   212→During fuzzing, syzkaller randomly picks one of the union options.\n   213→\n   214→You may also specify conditions that determine whether the corresponding\n   215→option may or may not be selected, depending on values of other fields. See\n   216→[the corresponding section](syscall_descriptions_syntax.md#conditional-fields)\n   217→for more details.\n   218→\n   219→Unions can have attributes specified in square brackets after the union.\n   220→Attributes are:\n   221→\n   222→- `varlen`: union size is the size of the particular chosen option (not statically known); without this attribute unions are statically sized as maximum of all options (similar to C unions)\n   223→- `size[N]`: the union is padded up to the specified size `N`; contents of the padding are unspecified (though, frequently are zeros)\n   224→\n   225→## Resources\n   226→\n   227→Resources represent values that need to be passed from output of one syscall to input of another syscall. For example, `close` syscall requires an input value (fd) previously returned by `open` or `pipe` syscall. To achieve this, `fd` is declared as a resource. This is a way of modelling dependencies between syscalls, as defining a syscall as the producer of a resource and another syscall as the consumer defines a loose sense of ordering between them. Resources are described as:\n   228→\n   229→```\n   230→\"resource\" identifier \"[\" underlying_type \"]\" [ \":\" const (\",\" const)* ]\n   231→```\n   232→\n   233→`underlying_type` is either one of `int8`, `int16`, `int32`, `int64`, `intptr` or another resource (which models inheritance, for example, a socket is a subtype of fd). The optional set of constants represent resource special values, for example, `0xffffffffffffffff` (-1) for \"no fd\", or `AT_FDCWD` for \"the current dir\". Special values are used once in a while as resource values. If no special values specified, special value of `0` is used. Resources can then be used as types, for example:\n   234→\n   235→```\n   236→resource fd[int32]: 0xffffffffffffffff, AT_FDCWD, 1000000\n   237→resource sock[fd]\n   238→resource sock_unix[sock]\n   239→\n   240→socket(...) sock\n   241→accept(fd sock, ...) sock\n   242→listen(fd sock, backlog int32)\n   243→```\n   244→\n   245→Resources don't have to be necessarily returned by a syscall. They can be used as any other data type. For example:\n   246→\n   247→```\n   248→resource my_resource[int32]\n   249→\n   250→request_producer(..., arg ptr[out, my_resource])\n   251→request_consumer(..., arg ptr[inout, test_struct])\n   252→\n   253→test_struct {\n   254→\t...\n   255→\tattr\tmy_resource\n   256→}\n   257→```\n   258→\n   259→For more complex producer/consumer scenarios, field attributes can be utilized.\n   260→For example:\n   261→\n   262→```\n   263→resource my_resource_1[int32]\n   264→resource my_resource_2[int32]\n   265→\n   266→request_produce1_consume2(..., arg ptr[inout, test_struct])\n   267→\n   268→test_struct {\n   269→\t...\n   270→\tfield0\tmy_resource_1\t(out)\n   271→\tfield1\tmy_resource_2\t(in)\n   272→}\n   273→```\n   274→\n   275→Each resource type must be \"produced\" (used as an output) by at least one syscall\n   276→(outside of unions and optional pointers) and \"consumed\" (used as an input)\n   277→by at least one syscall.\n   278→\n   279→## Type Aliases\n   280→\n   281→Complex types that are often repeated can be given short type aliases using the\n   282→following syntax:\n   283→\n   284→```\n   285→type identifier underlying_type\n   286→```\n   287→\n   288→For example:\n   289→\n   290→```\n   291→type signalno int32[0:65]\n   292→type net_port proc[20000, 4, int16be]\n   293→```\n   294→\n   295→Then, type alias can be used instead of the underlying type in any contexts.\n   296→Underlying type needs to be described as if it's a struct field, that is,\n   297→with the base type if it's required. However, type alias can be used as syscall\n   298→arguments as well. Underlying types are currently restricted to integer types,\n   299→`ptr`, `ptr64`, `const`, `flags` and `proc` types.\n   300→\n   301→There are some builtin type aliases:\n   302→\n   303→```\n   304→type bool8\tint8[0:1]\n   305→type bool16\tint16[0:1]\n   306→type bool32\tint32[0:1]\n   307→type bool64\tint64[0:1]\n   308→type boolptr\tintptr[0:1]\n   309→\n   310→type fileoff[BASE] BASE\n   311→\n   312→type filename string[filename]\n   313→\n   314→type buffer[DIR] ptr[DIR, array[int8]]\n   315→```\n   316→\n   317→## Type Templates\n   318→\n   319→Type templates can be declared as follows:\n   320→\n   321→```\n   322→type buffer[DIR] ptr[DIR, array[int8]]\n   323→type fileoff[BASE] BASE\n   324→type nlattr[TYPE, PAYLOAD] {\n   325→\tnla_len\t\tlen[parent, int16]\n   326→\tnla_type\tconst[TYPE, int16]\n   327→\tpayload\t\tPAYLOAD\n   328→} [align_4]\n   329→```\n   330→\n   331→and later used as follows:\n   332→\n   333→```\n   334→syscall(a buffer[in], b fileoff[int64], c ptr[in, nlattr[FOO, int32]])\n   335→```\n   336→\n   337→There is builtin type template `optional` defined as:\n   338→\n   339→```\n   340→type optional[T] [\n   341→\tval\tT\n   342→\tvoid\tvoid\n   343→] [varlen]\n   344→```\n   345→\n   346→## Length\n   347→\n   348→You can specify length of a particular field in struct or a named argument by\n   349→using `len`, `bytesize` and `bitsize` types, for example:\n   350→\n   351→```\n   352→write(fd fd, buf ptr[in, array[int8]], count len[buf])\n   353→\n   354→sock_fprog {\n   355→\tlen\tlen[filter, int16]\n   356→\tfilter\tptr[in, array[sock_filter]]\n   357→}\n   358→```\n   359→\n   360→If `len`'s argument is a pointer, then the length of the pointee argument is used.\n   361→\n   362→To denote the length of a field in N-byte words use `bytesizeN`, possible values\n   363→for N are 1, 2, 4 and 8.\n   364→\n   365→To denote the length of the parent struct, you can use `len[parent, int8]`.\n   366→To denote the length of the higher level parent when structs are embedded into\n   367→one another, you can specify the type name of the particular parent:\n   368→\n   369→```\n   370→s1 {\n   371→    f0      len[s2]  # length of s2\n   372→}\n   373→\n   374→s2 {\n   375→    f0      s1\n   376→    f1      array[int32]\n   377→    f2      len[parent, int32]\n   378→}\n   379→```\n   380→\n   381→`len` argument can also be a path expression which allows more complex\n   382→addressing. Path expressions are similar to C field references, but also allow\n   383→referencing parent and sibling elements. A special reference `syscall` used\n   384→in the beginning of the path allows to refer directly to the syscall arguments.\n   385→For example:\n   386→\n   387→```\n   388→s1 {\n   389→\ta\tptr[in, s2]\n   390→\tb\tptr[in, s3]\n   391→\tc\tarray[int8]\n   392→}\n   393→\n   394→s2 {\n   395→\td\tarray[int8]\n   396→}\n   397→\n   398→s3 {\n   399→# This refers to the array c in the parent s1.\n   400→\te\tlen[s1:c, int32]\n   401→# This refers to the array d in the sibling s2.\n   402→\tf\tlen[s1:a:d, int32]\n   403→# This refers to the array k in the child s4.\n   404→\tg\tlen[i:j, int32]\n   405→# This refers to syscall argument l.\n   406→\th\tlen[syscall:l, int32]\n   407→\ti\tptr[in, s4]\n   408→}\n   409→\n   410→s4 {\n   411→\tj\tarray[int8]\n   412→}\n   413→\n   414→foo(k ptr[in, s1], l ptr[in, array[int8]])\n   415→```\n   416→\n   417→## Proc\n   418→\n   419→The `proc` type can be used to denote per process integers.\n   420→The idea is to have a separate range of values for each executor, so they don't interfere.\n   421→\n   422→The simplest example is a port number.\n   423→The `proc[20000, 4, int16be]` type means that we want to generate an `int16be`\n   424→integer starting from `20000` and assign `4` values for each process.\n   425→As a result the executor number `n` will get values in the `[20000 + n * 4, 20000 + (n + 1) * 4)` range.\n   426→\n   427→## Integer Constants\n   428→\n   429→Integer constants can be specified as decimal literals, as `0x`-prefixed\n   430→hex literals, as `'`-surrounded char literals, or as symbolic constants\n   431→extracted from kernel headers or defined by `define` directives. For example:\n   432→\n   433→```\n   434→foo(a const[10], b const[-10])\n   435→foo(a const[0xabcd])\n   436→foo(a int8['a':'z'])\n   437→foo(a const[PATH_MAX])\n   438→foo(a int32[PATH_MAX])\n   439→foo(a ptr[in, array[int8, MY_PATH_MAX]])\n   440→define MY_PATH_MAX\tPATH_MAX + 2\n   441→```\n   442→\n   443→## Conditional fields\n   444→\n   445→### In structures\n   446→\n   447→In syzlang, it's possible to specify a condition for every struct field that\n   448→determines whether the field should be included or omitted:\n   449→\n   450→```\n   451→header_fields {\n   452→  magic       const[0xabcd, int16]\n   453→  haveInteger int8\n   454→} [packed]\n   455→\n   456→packet {\n   457→  header  header_fields\n   458→  integer int64  (if[value[header:haveInteger] == 0x1])\n   459→  body    array[int8]\n   460→} [packed]\n   461→\n   462→some_call(a ptr[in, packet])\n   463→```\n   464→\n   465→In this example, the `packet` structure will include the field `integer` only\n   466→if `header.haveInteger == 1`. In memory, `packet` will have the following\n   467→layout:\n   468→\n   469→| header.magic = 0xabcd | header.haveInteger = 0x1 | integer | body |\n   470→| --------------------- | ------------------------ | ------- | ---- |\n   471→\n   472→That corresponds to e.g. the following program:\n   473→\n   474→```\n   475→some_call(&AUTO={{AUTO, 0x1}, @value=0xabcd, []})\n   476→```\n   477→\n   478→If `header.haveInteger` is not `1`, syzkaller will just pretend that the field\n   479→`integer` does not exist.\n   480→\n   481→```\n   482→some_call(&AUTO={{AUTO, 0x0}, @void, []})\n   483→```\n   484→\n   485→| header.magic = 0xabcd | header.haveInteger = 0x0 | body |\n   486→| --------------------- | ------------------------ | ---- |\n   487→\n   488→Every conditional field is assumed to be of variable length and so is the struct\n   489→to which this field belongs.\n   490→\n   491→When a variable length field appears in the middle of a structure, the structure\n   492→must be marked with `[packed].`\n   493→\n   494→Conditions on bitfields are prohibited:\n   495→\n   496→```\n   497→struct {\n   498→  f0 int\n   499→  f1 int:3 (if[value[f0] == 0x1])  # It will not compile.\n   500→}\n   501→```\n   502→\n   503→But you may reference bitfields in your conditions:\n   504→\n   505→```\n   506→struct {\n   507→  f0 int:1\n   508→  f1 int:7\n   509→  f2 int   (if[value[f0] == value[f1]])\n   510→} [packed]\n   511→```\n   512→\n   513→### In unions\n   514→\n   515→Let's consider the following example.\n   516→\n   517→```\n   518→struct {\n   519→  type int\n   520→  body alternatives\n   521→}\n   522→\n   523→alternatives [\n   524→  int     int64 (if[value[struct:type] == 0x1])\n   525→  arr     array[int64, 5] (if[value[struct:type] == 0x2])\n   526→  default int32\n   527→] [varlen]\n   528→\n   529→some_call(a ptr[in, struct])\n   530→```\n   531→\n   532→In this case, the union option will be selected depending on the value of the\n   533→`type` field. For example, if `type` is `0x1`, then it can be either `int` or\n   534→`default`:\n   535→\n   536→```\n   537→some_call(&AUTO={0x1, @int=0x123})\n   538→some_call(&AUTO={0x1, @default=0x123})\n   539→```\n   540→\n   541→If `type` is `0x2`, it can be either `arr` or `default`.\n   542→\n   543→If `type` is neither `0x1` nor `0x2`, syzkaller may only select `default`:\n   544→\n   545→```\n   546→some_call(&AUTO={0x0, @default=0xabcd})\n   547→```\n   548→\n   549→To ensure that a union can always be constructed, the last union field **must always\n   550→have no condition**.\n   551→\n   552→Thus, the following definition would fail to compile:\n   553→\n   554→```\n   555→alternatives [\n   556→  int int64 (if[value[struct:type] == 0x1])\n   557→  arr array[int64, 5] (if[value[struct:type] == 0x1])\n   558→] [varlen]\n   559→```\n   560→\n   561→During prog mutation and generation syzkaller will select a random union field\n   562→whose condition is satisfied.\n   563→\n   564→### Expression syntax\n   565→\n   566→Currently, only `==`, `!=`, `&` and `||` operators are supported. However, the\n   567→functionality was designed in such a way that adding more operators is easy.\n   568→Feel free to file a GitHub issue or write us an email in case it's needed.\n   569→\n   570→Expressions are evaluated as `int64` values. If the final result of an\n   571→expression is not 0, it's assumed to be satisfied.\n   572→\n   573→If you want to reference a field's value, you can do it via\n   574→`value[path:to:field]`, which is similar to the `len[]` argument.\n   575→\n   576→```\n   577→sub_struct {\n   578→  f0 int\n   579→  # Reference a field in a parent struct.\n   580→  f1 int (if[value[struct:f2]]) # Same as if[value[struct:f2] != 0].\n   581→}\n   582→\n   583→struct {\n   584→  f2 int\n   585→  f3 sub_struct\n   586→  f4 int (if[value[f2] == 0x2]) # Reference a sibling field.\n   587→  f5 int (if[value[f3:f0] == 0x1]) # Reference a nested field.\n   588→  f6 int (if[value[f3:f0] == 0x1 || value[f3:f0] == 0x2]) # Reference a nested field which either equals to 0x1 or 0x2.\n   589→} [packed]\n   590→\n   591→call(a ptr[in, struct])\n   592→```\n   593→\n   594→The referenced field must be of integer type and there must be no\n   595→conditional fields in the path to it. For example, the following\n   596→descriptions will not compile.\n   597→\n   598→```\n   599→struct {\n   600→  f0 int\n   601→  f1 int (if[value[f0] == 0x1])\n   602→  f2 int (if[value[f1] == 0x1])\n   603→}\n   604→```\n   605→\n   606→You may also reference constants in expressions:\n   607→\n   608→```\n   609→struct {\n   610→  f0 int\n   611→  f1 int\n   612→  f2 int (if[value[f0] & SOME_CONST == OTHER_CONST])\n   613→}\n   614→```\n   615→\n   616→## Meta\n   617→\n   618→Description files can also contain `meta` directives that specify meta-information for the whole file.\n   619→\n   620→```\n   621→meta noextract\n   622→```\n   623→\n   624→Tells `make extract` to not extract constants for this file.\n   625→Though, `syz-extract` can still be invoked manually on this file.\n   626→\n   627→```\n   628→meta arches[\"arch1\", \"arch2\"]\n   629→```\n   630→\n   631→Restricts this file only to the given set of architectures.\n   632→`make extract` and `make generate` will not use it on other architectures.\n   633→\n   634→## Misc\n   635→\n   636→Description files also contain `include` directives that refer to Linux kernel header files,\n   637→`incdir` directives that refer to custom Linux kernel header directories\n   638→and `define` directives that define symbolic constant values.\n   639→\n   640→The syzkaller executor defines some [pseudo system calls](./pseudo_syscalls.md)\n   641→that can be used as any other syscall in a description file. These pseudo\n   642→system calls expand to literal C code and can perform user-defined\n   643→custom actions. You can find some examples in\n   644→[executor/common_linux.h](../executor/common_linux.h).\n   645→\n   646→Also see [tips](syscall_descriptions.md#tips) on writing good descriptions.\n   647→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_011GDxmRP236XWNmQ2BcDdhn","session_id":"c7773ed7-282a-474f-aba7-91cf68ca3551","uuid":"8a16a225-4f14-4333-8c6f-fd32a0393490"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01VzSNep23rXUeRza14yg77M","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Pseudo-syscalls\n     2→\n     3→Besides regular system calls, a [syscall\n     4→description](syscall_descriptions.md) file can also contain\n     5→pseudo-syscalls. These are C functions defined in the\n     6→executor. When a test program uses a pseudo-syscall, the executor\n     7→will generate the pseudo-syscall function code in the resulting C program.\n     8→\n     9→This allows a test program to have specific code blocks to perform\n    10→certain actions, they may also be used as more test-friendly wrappers\n    11→for primitive syscalls.\n    12→\n    13→Use of pseudo-syscalls is generally **discouraged** because they ruin all\n    14→advantages of the declarative descriptions (declarativeness, conciseness,\n    15→fuzzer control over all aspects, possibility of global improvements to\n    16→the logic, static checking, fewer bugs, etc), increase maintenance burden,\n    17→are non-reusable and make C reproducers longer. However, syzlang is not\n    18→expressive enough to cover all possible cases, so use of pseudo-syscalls\n    19→needs to be considered on a case-by-cases basis (additional benefit,\n    20→amount of code, possibility of extending syzlang to cover this case, etc).\n    21→\n    22→## How to add a pseudo-syscall to the executor\n    23→\n    24→First, think about the scope of the pseudo-syscall and which systems and\n    25→subsystems it will be related to. The executor includes a fixed set of C\n    26→header files `executor/common*.h` containing the code of the pseudo-syscalls.\n    27→Check if the new one can fit in one of the existing files before creating\n    28→a new one.\n    29→\n    30→For instance, if our new pseudo-syscall is Linux-specific, then\n    31→[common_linux.h](../executor/common_linux.h) would be the place to put it.\n    32→\n    33→The actual pseudo-syscall function may look something like this:\n    34→\n    35→    #if SYZ_EXECUTOR || __NR_syz_mycall\n    36→    /* Add all the necessary #include and #define headers */\n    37→\n    38→    static long syz_mycall(volatile long a0, volatile long a1)\n    39→    {\n    40→            /* Function body */\n    41→    }\n    42→    #endif\n    43→\n    44→Make sure that all the function requirements are met and that it can\n    45→be compiled. Note that the function name must start with \"syz_\". It may\n    46→also take a different number of arguments. Type of arguments must be\n    47→`volatile long`, return type - `long`. `long` is required to avoid\n    48→potential calling convention issues because it is casted to a function\n    49→pointer that accepts `long`'s. The reason for `volatile` is interesting:\n    50→lots of libc functions are annotated with various argument constraints\n    51→(e.g. this argument should not be `NULL`, or that argument must be a\n    52→valid file descriptor); C reproducers may call these functions with\n    53→constant arguments and compiler may see that some of these constraints\n    54→are violated (e.g. passing `NULL` to a `non-NULL` argument, or passing\n    55→`-1` as file descriptor) and produce errors/warnings. `volatile` prevents\n    56→that.\n    57→\n    58→Now, to handle the pseudo-syscall properly we have to update the\n    59→`linuxSyscallChecks` in\n    60→[linux_syscalls.go](../pkg/vminfo/linux_syscalls.go) and add a particular\n    61→case for this syscall, enabling it when necessary. If we want to enable\n    62→it unconditionally we can simply use `alwaysSupported` for it.\n    63→\n    64→Finally, run `make generate`. Now you can use it in a syscall\n    65→description file as if it was a regular system call:\n    66→\n    67→    syz_mycall(arg0 pid, arg1 const[0])\n    68→\n    69→<div id=\"dependencies\"/>\n    70→\n    71→## External Dependencies\n    72→\n    73→The implementation must not use any external libraries nor external headers,\n    74→except for the most basic and standard ones (like `<unistd.h>` and\n    75→`<sys/mman.h>`). In particular, it must not depend on libraries/headers\n    76→installed by additional packages nor on headers for recently added kernel\n    77→subsystems. External dependencies have proved to be brittle and easily cause\n    78→build breakage because all dependencies will be required for any build/run on\n    79→the fuzzer and any C reproducer. For example, packages/headers may be missing\n    80→on some distros, named differently, be of a wrong version, broken, or conflict\n    81→with other headers. Unfortunately, there is no way to reliably specify such\n    82→dependencies and requirements for C programs. Therefore, if the pseudo-syscall\n    83→requires definitions of some structures, constants, or helper functions, these\n    84→should be described in the executor code itself as minimally as possible (they\n    85→will be part of C reproducers).\n    86→\n    87→## Testing\n    88→\n    89→Each new pseudo-syscall should have at least one test in `sys/OS/test`.\n    90→See [Linux tests](/sys/linux/test) for an example. A tests is just a program\n    91→with checked syscall return values. There should be at least one test\n    92→that contains \"the main successful scenario\" of using the pseudo-syscall.\n    93→See [io_uring test](/sys/linux/test/io_uring) as a good example.\n    94→Such tests are important because they ensure that the pseudo-syscall code\n    95→does not contain \"stupid\" bugs (e.g. crash on NULL-deref each time),\n    96→that it is possible for the fuzzer to come up with the successful scenario\n    97→(as a combination of the pseudo-syscall and the surrounding descriptions)\n    98→and that it will continue to work in future.\n    99→See [Testing of descriptions](syscall_descriptions.md#testing)\n   100→for details about the tests.\n   101→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_011GDxmRP236XWNmQ2BcDdhn","session_id":"c7773ed7-282a-474f-aba7-91cf68ca3551","uuid":"aefd9c19-ba86-451c-86f8-c99dd9c50729"}

STRUCT AND UNION DEFINITIONS\n\n### Struct Syntax\n\n```\nstructname {\n    field1  type1\n    field2  type2  (in|out|inout)         # optional direction\n    field3  type3  (if[condition])        # optional condition\n} [(packed|align[N]|size[N])]\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ashmem.txt`:**\n```\nashmem_pin {\n    offset  int32\n    len     int32\n}\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt`:**\n```\ntermios {\n    c_iflag     int32\n    c_oflag     int32\n    c_cflag     int32\n    c_lflag     int32\n    c_line      int8[N_TTY:N_NULL]\n    c_cc        array[int8, NCCS]\n}\n```\n\n### Struct Attributes\n\n```\nstructname { ... } [packed]           # No padding, alignment 1\nstructname { ... } [align[8]]         # Aligned to 8 bytes\nstructname { ... } [size[256]]        # Padded to 256 bytes\n```\n\n### Union Syntax\n\n```\nunionname [\n    option1  type1  (if[condition])\n    option2  type2\n    default  type3\n] [(varlen|size[N])]\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_binder.txt`:**\n```\nbinder_object [\n    flat    flat_binder_object\n    fd      binder_fd_object\n    fda     binder_fd_array_object\n    ptr     binder_buffer_object\n] [varlen]\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. TYPE DEFINITIONS\n\n### Type Aliases\n\n```\ntype typename underlying_type\ntype signalno int32[0:65]\ntype net_port proc[20000, 4, int16be]\ntype filename string[filename]\n```\n\n### Type Templates\n\n```\ntype buffer[DIR] ptr[DIR, array[int8]]\ntype fileoff[BASE] BASE\ntype nlattr[TYPE, PAYLOAD] {\n    nla_len     len[parent, int16]\n    nla_type    const[TYPE, int16]\n    payload     PAYLOAD\n} [align_4]\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. TYPE SYSTEM\n\n### Base Types\n\n| Type | Size | Description |\n|------|------|-------------|\n| int8, int16, int32, int64 | varies | Fixed-size integers |\n| intptr | pointer-size | Long/pointer-sized integer |\n| int16be, int32be | varies | Big-endian integers |\n| int32:20 | variable | Bitfield (20-bit) |\n\n### Integer Ranges\n\n```\nint32[0:100]              # Range 0-100\nint32[1:10, 2]            # Values {1, 3, 5, 7, 9} (2-byte aligned)\nint8[10]                  # Constant value 10\n```\n\n### Pointer Types\n\n```\nptr[in, string]           # Input pointer to string\nptr[out, array[int32]]    # Output pointer to array\nptr[inout, struct]        # Input/output pointer\nptr64[in, data]           # 8-byte pointer (64-bit)\n```\n\n### String Types\n\n```\nstring[\"literal\"]         # Constant string\nstring[filename]          # Filename flags reference\nstring[\"/dev/foo\"]        # Device path\nstring[\"/dev/foo#\"]       # Device path with # replaced by number\nstringnoz[\"data\"]         # Non-zero-terminated string\n```\n\n### Special Types\n\n```\nconst[value, intN]        # Constant value\nflags[flagname, intN]     # Flags value\narray[elemtype]           # Variable-length array\narray[elemtype, 5]        # Fixed-length array (5 elements)\narray[elemtype, 5:10]     # Variable-length array (5-10 elements)\nlen[fieldname, intN]      # Length of field\nbytesize[fieldname, intN] # Byte size of field\nvma[N]                    # Virtual memory area (N pages)\nproc[20000, 4, int16]     # Per-process value\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. FLAGS DEFINITIONS\n\n### Syntax\n\n```\nflagname = CONST1, CONST2, CONST3, ...\nflagname = FLAG1 | FLAG2 | FLAG3\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt`:**\n```\ntcxonc_arg = TCOOFF, TCOON, TCIOFF, TCION\n\ntiocl_selection_mode = TIOCL_SELCHAR, TIOCL_SELWORD, TIOCL_SELLINE, \n                       TIOCL_SELPOINTER, TIOCL_SELCLEAR, \n                       TIOCL_SELMOUSEREPORT, TIOCL_SELBUTTONMASK\n```\n\n### String Flags\n\n```\nstring_flags = \"option1\", \"option2\", \"option3\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. SYZ_OPEN_DEV PSEUDO-SYSCALL\n\n### Purpose\nOpens device files with dynamic minor/major number substitution.\n\n### Implementation\n\nFrom `/opt/syzkaller/executor/common_linux.h` (lines 2411-2438):\n\n```c\nstatic long syz_open_dev(volatile long a0, volatile long a1, volatile long a2)\n{\n    if (a0 == 0xc || a0 == 0xb) {\n        // Character or block device\n        // a0 = 0xc (char) or 0xb (block)\n        // a1 = major number\n        // a2 = minor number\n        char buf[128];\n        sprintf(buf, \"/dev/%s/%d:%d\", \n                a0 == 0xc ? \"char\" : \"block\", \n                (uint8)a1, (uint8)a2);\n        return open(buf, O_RDWR, 0);\n    } else {\n        // String-based device\n        // a0 = pointer to device string with '#' placeholders\n        // a1 = number to substitute for '#'\n        // a2 = open flags\n        unsigned long nb = a1;\n        char buf[1024];\n        char* hash;\n        strncpy(buf, (char*)a0, sizeof(buf) - 1);\n        buf[sizeof(buf) - 1] = 0;\n        \n        // Replace each '#' with digits from a1\n        while ((hash = strchr(buf, '#'))) {\n            *hash = '0' + (char)(nb % 10);  // 10 devices max\n            nb /= 10;\n        }\n        return open(buf, a2 & ~O_CREAT, 0);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Variants\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_hidraw.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$hidraw(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/hidraw#\"]], \n                    id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_hidraw\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_i2c.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$I2C(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/i2c-#\"]], \n                 id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_i2c\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_rtc.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$rtc(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/rtc#\"]], \n                 id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_rtc\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_video4linux.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$video(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/video#\"]], \n                   id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_video\nsyz_open_dev$video4linux(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/v4l-subdev#\"]], \n                         id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_video\nsyz_open_dev$vivid(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/video#\"]], \n                   id proc[3, 4], flags const[O_RDWR]) fd_video\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_dri.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$dri(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/dri/card#\"]], \n                 id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_dri\nsyz_open_dev$dricontrol(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/dri/controlD#\"]], \n                        id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_dri\nsyz_open_dev$drirender(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/dri/renderD#\"]], \n                       id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_dri\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_snd_midi.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$sndmidi(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/snd/midiC#D#\"]], \n                     id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_midi\nsyz_open_dev$midi(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/midi#\"]], \n                  id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_midi\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$ttys(dev const[0xc], major const[2], minor proc[20, 2]) fd_tty\nsyz_open_dev$ptys(dev const[0xc], major const[3], minor proc[20, 2]) fd_tty\nsyz_open_dev$tty1(dev const[0xc], major const[4], minor intptr[1:4]) fd_tty\nsyz_open_dev$tty20(dev const[0xc], major const[4], minor proc[20, 2]) fd_tty\n```\n\n### Key Points\n\n1. **String-based variant**: Uses `#` as placeholder for number substitution\n   - Multiple `#` characters: rightmost replaced first, number divided by 10 each time\n   - Maximum 10 devices per number\n   \n2. **Character/block device variant**: Uses 0xc for character, 0xb for block\n   - Creates `/dev/char/major:minor` or `/dev/block/major:minor`\n\n3. **Number source**: Can be:\n   - `intptr` - fixed integer\n   - `proc[start, count]` - per-process value (process-specific)\n   - `int32[range]` - ranged value\n\n---\n\n## 9. IOCTL DESCRIPTIONS\n\n### Basic Syntax\n\n```\nioctl$NAME(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_CONSTANT], arg [type])\n```\n\n### Direction Attributes\n\n```\nioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[in, struct])      # Input\nioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[out, struct])     # Output\nioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[inout, struct])   # Input/Output\n```\n\n### Examples\n\n**From Return Types\n\n```\nioctl$NAME(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg type) fd    # Returns fd\nioctl$NAME(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg type)       # No specific return\n```\n\n---\n\n## 10. CONSTANT FILES (.txt.const)\n\n### Format\n\nThe `.txt.const` files are **generated** (DO NOT EDIT) and contain architecture-specific constant values.\n\n**Structure:**\n\n```\n# Code generated by syz-sysgen. DO NOT EDIT.\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nCONSTANT_NAME = value1, arch:value2, arch:value3\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_input.txt.const`:**\n```\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nAT_FDCWD = 18446744073709551516\nEVIOCGABS0 = 2149074240, mips64le:ppc64le:1075332416\nEVIOCGABS20 = 2149074272, mips64le:ppc64le:1075332448\nEVIOCGID = 2148025602, mips64le:ppc64le:1074283778\nEV_ABS = 3\nEV_KEY = 1\nFF_CONSTANT = 82\n__NR_ioctl = 54, amd64:16, arm64:riscv64:29, mips64le:5015\n__NR_write = 4, amd64:1, arm64:riscv64:64, mips64le:5001\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_snd_midi.txt.const`:**\n```\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_DRAIN = 1074026289, mips64le:ppc64le:2147768113\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_DROP = 1074026288, mips64le:ppc64le:2147768112\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_INFO = 2165069569, mips64le:ppc64le:1091327745\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_STREAM_INPUT = 1\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_STREAM_OUTPUT = 0\n__NR_ioctl = 54, amd64:16, arm64:riscv64:29, mips64le:5015\n```\n\n### Architecture Format\n\n- `CONST = value` - default for all arches\n- `CONST = value1, amd64:value2` - specific value for amd64\n- `CONST = value1, mips64le:ppc64le:value2` - same value for multiple arches\n- `CONST = value1, 386:arm:value2, amd64:value3` - multiple overrides\n\n---\n\n## 11. KEY CONCEPTS SUMMARY\n\n| Concept | Purpose | Example |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| **Resource** | Models syscall dependencies | `resource fd[int32]: -1` |\n| **syz_open_dev** | Opens devices with dynamic numbering | `syz_open_dev$rtc(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/rtc#\"]], ...)` |\n| **ioctl** | Device control operations | `ioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[in, type])` |\n| **Struct** | Fixed/variable-length data layout | `struct_name { field type, ... }` |\n| **Union** | Multiple type options | `union_name [ option1 type1, ... ] [varlen]` |\n| **Type Template** | Generic type definitions | `type buffer[DIR] ptr[DIR, array[int8]]` |\n| **Flags** | Bit flag combinations | `flagname = FLAG1, FLAG2, FLAG3` |\n| **Direction** | Input/output/inout specification | `ptr[in, type]`, `ptr[out, type]` |\n\n---\n\n## 14. Let me create a comprehensive research document:\n\n## Syzkaller Syzlang Description Language Format - Comprehensive Research\n\nBased on my analysis of the syzkaller codebase, here's a complete guide to understanding the syzlang description language format:\n\n---\n\n## 1. SYZLANG (.txt) FILE SYNTAX\n\n### File Structure\n\nA syzlang description file consists of:\n\n```\n[meta directives]\n[include directives]\n[define directives]\n[resource definitions]\n[syscall definitions]\n[struct/union definitions]\n[flags definitions]\n```\n\n### Meta Directives\n\n```\nmeta noextract              # Don't extract constants for this file\nmeta arches[\"arch1\", \"arch2\"]  # Restrict file to specific architectures\n```\n\n### Include Directives\n\n```\ninclude <linux/socket.h>\ninclude <uapi/linux/fcntl.h>\ninclude <drivers/staging/android/uapi/ashmem.h>\n```\n\n### Define Directives\n\n```\ndefine MY_CONST 100\ndefine I2C_MAX_BLOCK_1 (I2C_SMBUS_BLOCK_MAX + 1)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. RESOURCE DEFINITIONS\n\nResources represent values passed between syscalls (dependencies):\n\n```\nresource fd[int32]: -1                    # fd resource with special value -1\nresource fd_ashmem[fd]                    # inherits from fd\nresource binder_ptr[int64]: 0             # with default value 0\nresource sock[fd]                         # socket inherits from fd\nresource drm_gem_handle[int32]            # custom resource type\n\n# Multiple special values:\nresource fd[int32]: 0xffffffffffffffff, AT_FDCWD, 1000000\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_binder.txt`:**\n```\nresource fd_binder[fd]\nresource binder_ptr[int64]: 0\ntype binder_node int64[0:3]\ntype binder_handle int32[0:3]\ntype binder_cookie const[0, int64]\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. SYSCALL DEFINITIONS\n\n### Basic Syntax\n\n```\nsyscallname(arg1 type1, arg2 type2, ...) [return_type] [(attributes)]\n```\n\n### Examples\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ashmem.txt`:**\n```\nopenat$ashmem(fd const[AT_FDCWD], file ptr[in, string[\"/dev/ashmem\"]], \n              flags flags[open_flags], mode const[0]) fd_ashmem\n\nioctl$ASHMEM_SET_NAME(fd fd_ashmem, cmd const[ASHMEM_SET_NAME], arg ptr[in, string])\nioctl$ASHMEM_GET_SIZE(fd fd_ashmem, cmd const[ASHMEM_GET_SIZE], arg const[0])\n```\n\n### Call Attributes\n\n```\nsyscall_name(...) (disabled)              # Don't use in fuzzing\nsyscall_name(...) (timeout[500])          # Add 500ms execution timeout\nsyscall_name(...) (prog_timeout[1000])    # Add 1000ms timeout for whole program\nsyscall_name(...) (ignore_return)         # Ignore return value\nsyscall_name(...) (breaks_returns)        # Ignore all subsequent returns\nsyscall_name(...) (no_generate)           # Only use seed descriptions\nsyscall_name(...) (no_minimize)           # Don't modify in minimization\nsyscall_name(...) (no_squash)             # Don't pass squashed arguments\nsyscall_name(...) (snapshot)              # Enabled only in snapshot mode\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. STRUCT AND UNION DEFINITIONS\n\n### Struct Syntax\n\n```\nstructname {\n    field1  type1\n    field2  type2  (in|out|inout)         # optional direction\n    field3  type3  (if[condition])        # optional condition\n} [(packed|align[N]|size[N])]\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ashmem.txt`:**\n```\nashmem_pin {\n    offset  int32\n    len     int32\n}\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt`:**\n```\ntermios {\n    c_iflag     int32\n    c_oflag     int32\n    c_cflag     int32\n    c_lflag     int32\n    c_line      int8[N_TTY:N_NULL]\n    c_cc        array[int8, NCCS]\n}\n```\n\n### Struct Attributes\n\n```\nstructname { ... } [packed]           # No padding, alignment 1\nstructname { ... } [align[8]]         # Aligned to 8 bytes\nstructname { ... } [size[256]]        # Padded to 256 bytes\n```\n\n### Union Syntax\n\n```\nunionname [\n    option1  type1  (if[condition])\n    option2  type2\n    default  type3\n] [(varlen|size[N])]\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_binder.txt`:**\n```\nbinder_object [\n    flat    flat_binder_object\n    fd      binder_fd_object\n    fda     binder_fd_array_object\n    ptr     binder_buffer_object\n] [varlen]\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. TYPE DEFINITIONS\n\n### Type Aliases\n\n```\ntype typename underlying_type\ntype signalno int32[0:65]\ntype net_port proc[20000, 4, int16be]\ntype filename string[filename]\n```\n\n### Type Templates\n\n```\ntype buffer[DIR] ptr[DIR, array[int8]]\ntype fileoff[BASE] BASE\ntype nlattr[TYPE, PAYLOAD] {\n    nla_len     len[parent, int16]\n    nla_type    const[TYPE, int16]\n    payload     PAYLOAD\n} [align_4]\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. TYPE SYSTEM\n\n### Base Types\n\n| Type | Size | Description |\n|------|------|-------------|\n| int8, int16, int32, int64 | varies | Fixed-size integers |\n| intptr | pointer-size | Long/pointer-sized integer |\n| int16be, int32be | varies | Big-endian integers |\n| int32:20 | variable | Bitfield (20-bit) |\n\n### Integer Ranges\n\n```\nint32[0:100]              # Range 0-100\nint32[1:10, 2]            # Values {1, 3, 5, 7, 9} (2-byte aligned)\nint8[10]                  # Constant value 10\n```\n\n### Pointer Types\n\n```\nptr[in, string]           # Input pointer to string\nptr[out, array[int32]]    # Output pointer to array\nptr[inout, struct]        # Input/output pointer\nptr64[in, data]           # 8-byte pointer (64-bit)\n```\n\n### String Types\n\n```\nstring[\"literal\"]         # Constant string\nstring[filename]          # Filename flags reference\nstring[\"/dev/foo\"]        # Device path\nstring[\"/dev/foo#\"]       # Device path with # replaced by number\nstringnoz[\"data\"]         # Non-zero-terminated string\n```\n\n### Special Types\n\n```\nconst[value, intN]        # Constant value\nflags[flagname, intN]     # Flags value\narray[elemtype]           # Variable-length array\narray[elemtype, 5]        # Fixed-length array (5 elements)\narray[elemtype, 5:10]     # Variable-length array (5-10 elements)\nlen[fieldname, intN]      # Length of field\nbytesize[fieldname, intN] # Byte size of field\nvma[N]                    # Virtual memory area (N pages)\nproc[20000, 4, int16]     # Per-process value\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. FLAGS DEFINITIONS\n\n### Syntax\n\n```\nflagname = CONST1, CONST2, CONST3, ...\nflagname = FLAG1 | FLAG2 | FLAG3\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt`:**\n```\ntcxonc_arg = TCOOFF, TCOON, TCIOFF, TCION\n\ntiocl_selection_mode = TIOCL_SELCHAR, TIOCL_SELWORD, TIOCL_SELLINE, \n                       TIOCL_SELPOINTER, TIOCL_SELCLEAR, \n                       TIOCL_SELMOUSEREPORT, TIOCL_SELBUTTONMASK\n```\n\n### String Flags\n\n```\nstring_flags = \"option1\", \"option2\", \"option3\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 8. SYZ_OPEN_DEV PSEUDO-SYSCALL\n\n### Purpose\nOpens device files with dynamic minor/major number substitution.\n\n### Implementation\n\nFrom `/opt/syzkaller/executor/common_linux.h` (lines 2411-2438):\n\n```c\nstatic long syz_open_dev(volatile long a0, volatile long a1, volatile long a2)\n{\n    if (a0 == 0xc || a0 == 0xb) {\n        // Character or block device\n        // a0 = 0xc (char) or 0xb (block)\n        // a1 = major number\n        // a2 = minor number\n        char buf[128];\n        sprintf(buf, \"/dev/%s/%d:%d\", \n                a0 == 0xc ? \"char\" : \"block\", \n                (uint8)a1, (uint8)a2);\n        return open(buf, O_RDWR, 0);\n    } else {\n        // String-based device\n        // a0 = pointer to device string with '#' placeholders\n        // a1 = number to substitute for '#'\n        // a2 = open flags\n        unsigned long nb = a1;\n        char buf[1024];\n        char* hash;\n        strncpy(buf, (char*)a0, sizeof(buf) - 1);\n        buf[sizeof(buf) - 1] = 0;\n        \n        // Replace each '#' with digits from a1\n        while ((hash = strchr(buf, '#'))) {\n            *hash = '0' + (char)(nb % 10);  // 10 devices max\n            nb /= 10;\n        }\n        return open(buf, a2 & ~O_CREAT, 0);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Variants\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_hidraw.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$hidraw(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/hidraw#\"]], \n                    id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_hidraw\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_i2c.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$I2C(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/i2c-#\"]], \n                 id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_i2c\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_rtc.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$rtc(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/rtc#\"]], \n                 id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_rtc\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_video4linux.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$video(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/video#\"]], \n                   id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_video\nsyz_open_dev$video4linux(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/v4l-subdev#\"]], \n                         id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_video\nsyz_open_dev$vivid(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/video#\"]], \n                   id proc[3, 4], flags const[O_RDWR]) fd_video\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_dri.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$dri(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/dri/card#\"]], \n                 id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_dri\nsyz_open_dev$dricontrol(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/dri/controlD#\"]], \n                        id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_dri\nsyz_open_dev$drirender(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/dri/renderD#\"]], \n                       id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_dri\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_snd_midi.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$sndmidi(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/snd/midiC#D#\"]], \n                     id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_midi\nsyz_open_dev$midi(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/midi#\"]], \n                  id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_midi\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt`:**\n```\nsyz_open_dev$ttys(dev const[0xc], major const[2], minor proc[20, 2]) fd_tty\nsyz_open_dev$ptys(dev const[0xc], major const[3], minor proc[20, 2]) fd_tty\nsyz_open_dev$tty1(dev const[0xc], major const[4], minor intptr[1:4]) fd_tty\nsyz_open_dev$tty20(dev const[0xc], major const[4], minor proc[20, 2]) fd_tty\n```\n\n### Key Points\n\n1. **String-based variant**: Uses `#` as placeholder for number substitution\n   - Multiple `#` characters: rightmost replaced first, number divided by 10 each time\n   - Maximum 10 devices per number\n   \n2. **Character/block device variant**: Uses 0xc for character, 0xb for block\n   - Creates `/dev/char/major:minor` or `/dev/block/major:minor`\n\n3. **Number source**: Can be:\n   - `intptr` - fixed integer\n   - `proc[start, count]` - per-process value (process-specific)\n   - `int32[range]` - ranged value\n\n---\n\n## 9. IOCTL DESCRIPTIONS\n\n### Basic Syntax\n\n```\nioctl$NAME(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_CONSTANT], arg [type])\n```\n\n### Direction Attributes\n\n```\nioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[in, struct])      # Input\nioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[out, struct])     # Output\nioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[inout, struct])   # Input/Output\n```\n\n### Examples\n\n**From Return Types\n\n```\nioctl$NAME(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg type) fd    # Returns fd\nioctl$NAME(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg type)       # No specific return\n```\n\n---\n\n## 10. CONSTANT FILES (.txt.const)\n\n### Format\n\nThe `.txt.const` files are **generated** (DO NOT EDIT) and contain architecture-specific constant values.\n\n**Structure:**\n\n```\n# Code generated by syz-sysgen. DO NOT EDIT.\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nCONSTANT_NAME = value1, arch:value2, arch:value3\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_input.txt.const`:**\n```\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nAT_FDCWD = 18446744073709551516\nEVIOCGABS0 = 2149074240, mips64le:ppc64le:1075332416\nEVIOCGABS20 = 2149074272, mips64le:ppc64le:1075332448\nEVIOCGID = 2148025602, mips64le:ppc64le:1074283778\nEV_ABS = 3\nEV_KEY = 1\nFF_CONSTANT = 82\n__NR_ioctl = 54, amd64:16, arm64:riscv64:29, mips64le:5015\n__NR_write = 4, amd64:1, arm64:riscv64:64, mips64le:5001\n```\n\n**From `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_snd_midi.txt.const`:**\n```\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_DRAIN = 1074026289, mips64le:ppc64le:2147768113\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_DROP = 1074026288, mips64le:ppc64le:2147768112\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_INFO = 2165069569, mips64le:ppc64le:1091327745\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_STREAM_INPUT = 1\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_STREAM_OUTPUT = 0\n__NR_ioctl = 54, amd64:16, arm64:riscv64:29, mips64le:5015\n```\n\n### Architecture Format\n\n- `CONST = value` - default for all arches\n- `CONST = value1, amd64:value2` - specific value for amd64\n- `CONST = value1, mips64le:ppc64le:value2` - same value for multiple arches\n- `CONST = value1, 386:arm:value2, amd64:value3` - multiple overrides\n\n---\n\n## 11. KEY CONCEPTS SUMMARY\n\n| Concept | Purpose | Example |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| **Resource** | Models syscall dependencies | `resource fd[int32]: -1` |\n| **syz_open_dev** | Opens devices with dynamic numbering | `syz_open_dev$rtc(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/rtc#\"]], ...)` |\n| **ioctl** | Device control operations | `ioctl$CMD(fd fd, cmd const[CMD], arg ptr[in, type])` |\n| **Struct** | Fixed/variable-length data layout | `struct_name { field type, ... }` |\n| **Union** | Multiple type options | `union_name [ option1 type1, ... ] [varlen]` |\n| **Type Template** | Generic type definitions | `type buffer[DIR] ptr[DIR, array[int8]]` |\n| **Flags** | Bit flag combinations | `flagname = FLAG1, FLAG2, FLAG3` |\n| **Direction** | Input/output/inout specification | `ptr[in, type]`, `ptr[out, type]` |\n\n---\n\n## 14. FILES REFERENCED\n\nAbsolute paths to key files:\n\n1. **Documentation:**\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/docs/syscall_descriptions_syntax.md` - Complete syzlang syntax reference\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/docs/pseudo_syscalls.md` - Info on pseudo-syscalls like syz_open_dev\n\n2. **Example Device Files:**\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ashmem.txt` - Simple device example\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_rtc.txt` - Device with syz_open_dev\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_ptmx.txt` - Complex ioctl definitions\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_binder.txt` - Complex structures and unions\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_dri.txt` - DRM ioctls with templates\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_video4linux.txt` - Multiple device variants\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_i2c.txt` - I2C protocol descriptions\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_hidraw.txt` - HID device example\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_snd_midi.txt` - Sound MIDI device\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_comedi.txt` - COMEDI device with flags\n\n3. **Constant Files:**\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_input.txt.const` - Architecture-specific ioctl constants\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_snd_midi.txt.const` - Audio device constants\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_dri.txt.const` - DRM constants\n\n4. **Implementation:**\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/executor/common_linux.h` (lines 2411-2438) - syz_open_dev C implementation\n   - `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/sys.txt` - Base syscall definitions\n\nThis comprehensive guide covers all aspects of syzlang including syntax, type system, device descriptions, ioctl specifications, and practical patterns used throughout the syzkaller codebase."}],"totalDurationMs":100792,"totalTokens":70818,"totalToolUseCount":28,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":787,"cache_read_input_tokens":70030,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":787,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1}}}

Include directives\n2. Define directives\n3. Resource definitions\n4. Syscall/ioctl definitions\n5. Struct/union definitions\n6. Flags definitions\n\nConstant files (`.txt.const`) contain architecture-specific constant values.\n\n## Include Directives\n\n```syzlang\ninclude <linux/ppdev.h>\ninclude <uapi/linux/fcntl.h>\n```\n\n## Define Directives\n\n```syzlang\ndefine MY_CONST 100\ndefine COMPUTED (SOME_CONST + 1)\n```\n\n## Resource Definitions\n\nResources model dependencies between syscalls:\n\n```syzlang\nresource fd[int32]: -1                    # fd with special value -1\nresource fd_ppdev[fd]                     # inherits from fd\nresource my_handle[int32]: 0              # with default value 0\n```\n\n## Syscall Definitions\n\n### Basic Syntax\n\n```syzlang\nsyscall_name(arg1 type1, arg2 type2) return_type\n```\n\n### Device Opener with syz_open_dev\n\nFor devices with numbered instances (e.g., /dev/parport0, /dev/parport1):\n\n```syzlang\nsyz_open_dev$parport(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/parport#\"]], id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_ppdev\n```\n\nThe `#` character is replaced with the number from `id` parameter.\n\n### Ioctl Definitions\n\n```syzlang\n# Read ioctl (kernel writes to user)\nioctl$CMD_READ(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_NAME], arg ptr[out, type])\n\n# Write ioctl (user writes to kernel)\nioctl$CMD_WRITE(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_NAME], arg ptr[in, type])\n\n# Read/write ioctl\nioctl$CMD_RW(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_NAME], arg ptr[inout, type])\n\n# Ioctl with no argument\nioctl$CMD_NONE(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_NAME], arg const[0])\n\n# Ioctl with integer argument\nioctl$CMD_INT(fd fd_type, cmd const[CMD_NAME], arg intptr)\n```\n\n## Direction Attributes\n\n- `in` - Data flows from user to kernel\n- `out` - Data flows from kernel to user\n- `inout` - Bidirectional data flow\n\n## Pointer Types\n\n```syzlang\nptr[in, type]             # Input pointer\nptr[out, type]            # Output pointer\nptr[inout, type]          # Input/output pointer\n```\n\n## Struct Definitions\n\n```syzlang\nstruct_name {\n    field1  int8\n    field2  int32\n    field3  ptr[in, array[int8]]\n}\n\n# Packed struct (no padding)\nstruct_name { ... } [packed]\n\n# Aligned struct\nstruct_name { ... } [align[8]]\n```\n\n## Flags Definitions\n\n```syzlang\nflag_name = FLAG1, FLAG2, FLAG3\n```\n\n## Type System\n\n### Base Types\n\n| Type | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| int8, int16, int32, int64 | Fixed-size integers |\n| intptr | Pointer-sized integer |\n| const[VALUE, TYPE] | Constant value |\n| flags[FLAGNAME, TYPE] | Flags combination |\n| array[TYPE] | Variable-length array |\n| array[TYPE, N] | Fixed-length array |\n\n### Integer Ranges\n\n```syzlang\nint32[0:100]              # Range 0-100\nint8[10]                  # Constant value 10\n```\n\n## Constant Files (.txt.const)\n\nFormat for architecture-specific constants:\n\n```\narches = 386, amd64\nCONSTANT_NAME = value_for_all\nARCH_SPECIFIC = value1, amd64:value2\n```\n\nIoctl numbers differ between architectures due to different struct sizes and ioctl encoding. Common patterns:\n- `386` and `arm` often share values\n- `amd64`, `arm64`, `riscv64` often share values\n- `mips64le` and `ppc64le` often share values\n\n## Example: Complete Device Description\n\n```syzlang\ninclude <linux/mydevice.h>\n\nresource fd_mydev[fd]\n\nsyz_open_dev$mydev(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/mydev#\"]], id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_mydev\n\nioctl$MYDEV_GET_INFO(fd fd_mydev, cmd const[MYDEV_GET_INFO], arg ptr[out, mydev_info])\nioctl$MYDEV_SET_MODE(fd fd_mydev, cmd const[MYDEV_SET_MODE], arg ptr[in, int32])\nioctl$MYDEV_CLAIM(fd fd_mydev, cmd const[MYDEV_CLAIM], arg const[0])\n\nmydev_info {\n    version     int32\n    flags       int32\n}\n\nmydev_modes = MODE_A, MODE_B, MODE_C\n```\n\n## References\n\n- `/opt/syzkaller/docs/syscall_descriptions_syntax.md` - Full syntax reference\n- `/opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_*.txt` - Example device descriptions\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":350,"cache_read_input_tokens":33468,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":350,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c7773ed7-282a-474f-aba7-91cf68ca3551","uuid":"08879aec-5b99-4a69-9c40-7137c4b9e72c"}

Linux Parallel Port Device Driver (ppdev)\n\nThe ppdev driver provides user-space access to parallel ports via /dev/parport* device files.\n\n## Device Files\n\n- `/dev/parport0`, `/dev/parport1`, etc. - One per parallel port\n\n## Header Files\n\n- `<linux/ppdev.h>` - ppdev ioctl definitions\n- `<linux/parport.h>` - Parallel port constants (IEEE1284 modes, etc.)\n\n## Ioctls (23 total from ppdev.h)\n\n### Port Claim/Release (no argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPCLAIM | none | Claim port for exclusive access |\n| PPRELEASE | none | Release claimed port |\n| PPYIELD | none | Yield port temporarily |\n| PPEXCL | none | Register for exclusive access (before PPCLAIM) |\n\n### Mode/Phase Control (int argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPSETMODE | write | Set IEEE1284 mode (e.g., IEEE1284_MODE_EPP) |\n| PPNEGOT | write | Negotiate IEEE1284 mode |\n| PPSETPHASE | write | Set IEEE1284 phase |\n| PPDATADIR | write | Set data direction (0=output, non-zero=input) |\n| PPGETMODE | read | Get current mode |\n| PPGETPHASE | read | Get current phase |\n| PPGETMODES | read | Get available hardware modes |\n\n### Data/Control/Status (unsigned char argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPRDATA | read | Read data register |\n| PPWDATA | write | Write data register |\n| PPRCONTROL | read | Read control register |\n| PPWCONTROL | write | Write control register |\n| PPRSTATUS | read | Read status register |\n| PPWCTLONIRQ | write | Set control lines on interrupt |\n\n### Frob Control (struct argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPFCONTROL | write | Frob control lines (mask + value) |\n\n### Interrupt Handling (int argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPCLRIRQ | read | Clear and return interrupt count |\n\n### Timeout (struct timeval argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPGETTIME | read | Get port timeout |\n| PPSETTIME | write | Set port timeout |\n\n### Flags (int argument)\n| Ioctl | Direction | Description |\n|-------|-----------|-------------|\n| PPGETFLAGS | read | Get current flags |\n| PPSETFLAGS | write | Set flags |\n\n## Structures\n\n### ppdev_frob_struct\n```c\nstruct ppdev_frob_struct {\n    unsigned char mask;  // Bits to modify\n    unsigned char val;   // New values for masked bits\n};\n```\n\n## IEEE1284 Mode Flags (from parport.h)\n\n```c\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_NIBBLE    0\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_BYTE      (1<<0)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_COMPAT    (1<<8)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_BECP      (1<<9)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_ECP       (1<<4)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_ECPRLE    (IEEE1284_MODE_ECP | (1<<5))\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_ECPSWE    (1<<10)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_EPP       (1<<6)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_EPPSL     (1<<11)\n#define IEEE1284_MODE_EPPSWE    (1<<12)\n#define IEEE1284_DEVICEID       (1<<2)\n#define IEEE1284_EXT_LINK       (1<<14)\n#define IEEE1284_ADDR           (1<<13)\n#define IEEE1284_DATA           0\n```\n\n## ppdev Flags (from ppdev.h)\n\n```c\n#define PP_FASTWRITE    (1<<2)\n#define PP_FASTREAD     (1<<3)\n#define PP_W91284PIC    (1<<4)\n#define PP_FLAGMASK     (PP_FASTWRITE | PP_FASTREAD | PP_W91284PIC)\n```\n\n## Parport Mode Flags (hardware capabilities)\n\n```c\n#define PARPORT_MODE_PCSPP      (1<<0)\n#define PARPORT_MODE_TRISTATE   (1<<1)\n#define PARPORT_MODE_EPP        (1<<2)\n#define PARPORT_MODE_ECP        (1<<3)\n#define PARPORT_MODE_COMPAT     (1<<4)\n#define PARPORT_MODE_DMA        (1<<5)\n#define PARPORT_MODE_SAFEININT  (1<<6)\n```\n\n## Ioctl Number Calculation\n\nLinux ioctl numbers are encoded as:\n- `_IO(type, nr)` - no data transfer\n- `_IOR(type, nr, size)` - read (kernel to user)\n- `_IOW(type, nr, size)` - write (user to kernel)\n- `_IOWR(type, nr, size)` - read/write\n\nFor ppdev: `type = 'p'` (0x70)\n\nExample calculations (for amd64):\n- `PPCLAIM = _IO('p', 0x8b) = 0x0000708b`\n- `PPRSTATUS = _IOR('p', 0x81, unsigned char) = 0x80017081`\n- `PPWDATA = _IOW('p', 0x86, unsigned char) = 0x40017086`\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":324,"cache_read_input_tokens":35319,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":324,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c7773ed7-282a-474f-aba7-91cf68ca3551","uuid":"41f7ff12-59b3-491c-b406-321fd38a6da4"}
